Exceptionally, the mandatory initial session may be attended, by virtue of a power of attorney, solely by the legal representative of a party whose physical presence is demonstrably not possible, in particular where the party faces difficulty travelling due to serious illness, or where videoconferencing via computer or another videoconferencing system is not available, or where the party resides abroad.

  6. The court seised of the dispute may impose on a party who failed to attend the mandatory initial mediation session, despite having been duly summoned to do so under this article, a fine of not less than one hundred (100) euros and not more than five hundred (500) euros, taking into account the party's overall conduct and the reasons for non-attendance. Fines under the preceding sentence accrue to the Legal Aid and Judicial Fund (ΤΑ.Χ.ΔΙ.Κ.), to which a copy of the decision is notified by the Court registrar, and may be adjusted by decision of the Minister of Justice. An appeal against the decision, as regards the fine, is not admissible unless it also concerns the merits of the case. A joint decision of the Ministers of Justice and Finance shall determine the procedure for assessing and collecting the fine and shall regulate any other related matter concerning the application of this paragraph. That procedure also applies to fines already imposed.

  7. If the parties decide to continue the mediation procedure, either with the same or with a different mediator, a written agreement to submit the dispute to mediation is drawn up and the procedure under Article 5 of this law is followed, which must be completed within forty (40) days, calculated from the day following the expiry of the above twenty- or thirty-day period. The parties may agree to extend the forty (40)-day period. The period from 1 to 31 August is not counted within the above time limits.

Article 8 – Enforceability of agreements resulting from mediation

  1. The mediator draws up a mediation record which must contain: a) the full name and tax identification number of the mediator; b) the date and place where the mediation took place; c) the full particulars of the parties who had recourse to mediation and the names of their legal representatives; d) a reference to the agreement, or the specific manner in which the parties had recourse to mediation, as provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 4 of this law; e) the full particulars of any other persons who took part in any way in the mediation proceedings; and f) the agreement reached by the parties during mediation, or a statement that no agreement was reached.

  2. At the end of the mediation proceedings, the record is signed by the mediator, the parties and their legal representatives. Where no agreement is reached, the record may be signed by the mediator alone. Either party may file the record of the agreement reached at any time with the registry of the court having subject-matter and territorial jurisdiction, before which the case is pending or is to be brought. After the record is filed with the Court, the bringing of an action for the same dispute is inadmissible to the extent that its subject matter is covered by the parties' agreement, and any pending proceedings are terminated. On filing, a fee of fifty (50.00) euros must be paid, the amount of which may be adjusted by joint decision of the Ministers of Finance and Justice. The cost of the fee is borne by the party filing the record, unless the parties agree otherwise.

  3. The mediation record under this article constitutes, from the time of its filing with the registry of the competent court under paragraph 2, an enforceable title pursuant to point (ζ) of paragraph 2 of Article 904 of the Code of Civil Procedure, provided the agreement may be the subject of enforced execution. The enforceable copy is issued free of charge by the judge or the president of the court competent under paragraph 2.

  4. Where the agreement contained in the mediation record also includes provisions concerning legal transactions which, by law, must be executed in notarial form, such transactions must be given notarial form. In that case, the rules governing the drawing up of such notarial deeds and their registration apply.

  5. The mediation record under this article, from the time of its filing with the registry of the competent court under paragraph 2, may be used as a title for the registration or removal of a mortgage, pursuant to point (c) of paragraph 1 of Article 293 of the Code of Civil Procedure.

Article 9 – Effects of mediation on limitation periods, forfeiture periods and procedural time limits

  1. The mediator's written notice to the parties of the holding of the mandatory initial session, or the agreement to have voluntary recourse to mediation under Article 5, suspends the running of limitation and forfeiture periods for bringing claims and exercising rights, provided that these had already begun to run under substantive law, as well as the procedural time limits under Articles 237 and 238 of the Code of Civil Procedure, for as long as the mediation proceedings last.

  2. Without prejudice to the provisions of Articles 261, 262 and 263 of the Civil Code, limitation and forfeiture periods for bringing claims and exercising rights under substantive law that were suspended resume running on the day following the drawing up of the record of failure to reach agreement, or the service of a notice of withdrawal from the mediation proceedings by one party to the other and to the mediator, or the completion or termination, in any manner, of the mediation proceedings. Where the agreement contained in the mediation record includes conditions, time limits, or any other term on which the exercise of the rights arising from the agreement depends, the suspended limitation and forfeiture periods resume running from the fulfilment of the condition or term, or the lapse of the time limit.

  3. The procedural time limits referred to in paragraph 1 resume running from the drawing up of the record of failure to reach agreement, or from the service of a notice of withdrawal from the mediation proceedings by one party to the other and to the mediator, or from the completion or termination, in any manner, of the mediation proceedings. Where agreement is reached, the resumption of procedural time limits is warranted for matters of the case not covered by the parties' agreement.

Article 10 – Central Mediation Committee

  1. The Central Mediation Committee is composed of thirteen (13) members and is constituted, by decision of the Minister of Justice, of:

a) two (2) judicial officers of the civil and criminal justice system, one (1) of whom holding at least the rank of Justice of the Supreme Court (Areios Pagos) or equivalent, serving or honorary, and one (1) holding at least the rank of President of a Court of First Instance, Court of Appeal judge or President of a Court of Appeal or equivalent, with experience or specialisation and preferably accredited in mediation, appointed after the opinion of the Supreme Judicial Council, where serving;

b) two (2) professors of higher education institutions, serving or emeritus, with experience or specialisation in mediation and preferably accredited, at least one of whom must have served as a professor of a Law School in the country;

c) two (2) representatives of the Plenary Assembly of the bar associations of the country, with experience or specialisation in mediation and preferably accredited, following the consenting opinion of the Plenary Assembly of Bar Associations;

d) two (2) representatives of the Ministry of Justice, from among staff serving in the Central Service holding at least grade Β΄, or from among seconded lawyers serving in the political office of the Minister or Deputy Minister of Justice or of the Secretary General;

e) two (2) mediators representing professional bodies of the country, following a call for expressions of interest published on the website of the Ministry of Justice;

f) three (3) mediators, following a call for expressions of interest published on the website of the Ministry of Justice, having at least four years' professional experience in their field and experience in mediation, arising either from participation in the training of mediators, or from participation in mediation proceedings as mediators, mediator assistants or legal representatives, or from participation in conferences, seminars and research programmes related to mediation.

  1. The President of the Central Mediation Committee is the judicial officer of the highest rank and, among those of equal rank, the most senior.

  2. Under the decision referred to in paragraph 1, an alternate member is designated for each regular member of the Central Mediation Committee.

  3. Members of the Central Mediation Committee, as well as third persons appointed as members of the sub-committee under point (d) of paragraph 3 of Article 11 of this law, are prohibited from maintaining any cooperative relationship with Training Bodies under this law.

  4. The term of office of the President and the members is three years and may be renewed once. The President and members of the Central Mediation Committee shall be replaced where they have been appointed in breach of paragraph 4.

  5. The duties of Secretary of the Central Mediation Committee are performed by one or more employees of the Central Service of the Ministry of Justice, or of the Courts, or of another public service or legal person governed by public law, who are made available to, or seconded on a full-time and exclusive basis from their service to, the Central Mediation Committee. For that secondment, a decision of the Minister of Justice is issued, notwithstanding any other provision, upon his or her request, following the opinion of the President of the Central Mediation Committee and the consenting opinion of the service council competent for the employee concerned.

Article 11 – Competences of the Central Mediation Committee

The Central Mediation Committee has competence to deal with every matter concerning the monitoring of the application of the institution of mediation.

The Central Mediation Committee may establish, at its discretion, sub-committees for the swift resolution and supervision of matters arising from the application of this law. Such sub-committees are composed of members of the Central Mediation Committee, with no restriction on a member participating in more than one sub-committee. These sub-committees are expressly authorised by the Central Mediation Committee to finally settle the matters they undertake, unless this law specifically provides that the Plenary Assembly of the Central Mediation Committee is competent.

In any event, four (4) sub-committees are compulsorily established within the Central Mediation Committee, with a two-year term, having the following competences:

a) the "Mediators' Register Committee", competent for keeping the Registers under Article 29 of this law, for every related matter or the issuing of acts concerning the Registers kept, and for collecting the annual Activity Reports pursuant to Article 21 of this law;

b) the "Ethics and Disciplinary Control Committee", competent for the compliance of mediators with the obligations arising from this law and for the application of disciplinary law and the imposition of disciplinary sanctions. This sub-committee is constituted pursuant to paragraph Ε.1 of Article 17 of this law;

c) the "Training Bodies Supervision Committee", competent for every matter concerning Mediator Training Bodies;

d) the "Examinations Committee", competent and responsible for conducting the written and oral examinations and grading candidates for the purpose of accrediting prospective mediators. The Examinations Committee consists of three (3) members drawn from the Central Mediation Committee, with an equal number of alternates, at least one of whom must be a judicial officer, who chairs the Committee. The Examinations Committee may, depending on the total number of candidates on each occasion, appoint two (2) additional members without alternates, who must hold the status of accredited mediator.

The composition of the above sub-committees and the number of members comprising them are determined by the Central Mediation Committee. In assessing the permissibility of the above members' participation in the sub-committees, account is taken, among other things, of matters relating to conflicts of interest and of competence of such members with other provisions and regulations in force governing their main profession.

The Central Mediation Committee submits to the Ministry of Justice, in the first ten days of December of each year, an Annual Report on the monitoring of the application of the institution, accompanied by proposals for its improvement.

Article 12 – Qualifications of mediators

  1. Mediators must be: a) graduates of tertiary education or holders of an equivalent qualification from a foreign institution of recognised standing; b) trained by a Mediator Training Body recognised by the Central Mediation Committee, or holders of an accreditation qualification from another Member State of the European Union; and c) accredited by the Central Mediation Committee and registered in the Registers under Article 29 of this law.

A holder of a doctoral degree from a Greek higher-education institution, or an equivalent foreign qualification, in the field of mediation is not required to undergo further training with a Mediator Training Body in order to be accredited, and may participate directly in the examinations for accreditation, in accordance with Article 28 of this law. Excluded from practising the profession of mediator are those serving as public, municipal or judicial employees, or employees of legal persons and institutions governed by public law, as well as serving judicial officers or public officials. Public employees and employees of legal persons governed by public law may act as accredited mediators solely within the scope of, and for the needs of, their service. Excluded from this prohibition are public officials who lawfully and concurrently practise a liberal profession.

  1. A mediator undertakes duties only if, in his or her judgment, he or she is able to meet the requirements of the specific mediation procedure, in accordance with his or her professional training, practical experience and skills.

  2. The mediator conducts mediation in accordance with the ethical provisions of this law and the European Code of Conduct for Mediators. The mediator may advertise the services offered, provided that he or she acts in a professional, honest and dignified manner.

Article 13 – Impartiality – Independence – Neutrality

  1. The mediator is obliged to act towards the parties free from personal judgments, beliefs and prejudices, and to ensure the equal participation of, and provide facilitation to, all parties within the mediation proceedings. The mediator is obliged not to undertake the conduct of a mediation, and, if already engaged, not to continue it, before disclosing to the parties any elements or facts that may affect, or give the impression of affecting, his or her impartiality and independence. Exceptionally, in such a case the mediator may undertake or continue to perform mediation duties only with the express consent of the parties and provided he or she is certain of being able to conduct the mediation with guarantees of impartiality and independence.

  2. The mediator may not direct the parties or impose on them the solution he or she favours. The mediator may express a personal opinion, which is not binding, only if the parties so wish, and is obliged to remain neutral as to the outcome of the mediation.

Article 14 – Conflict of interest

  1. The mediator is obliged not to undertake duties, and, if already engaged, not to continue performing them, in the event of a conflict of interest, disclosing the relevant impediment to the parties.

Exceptionally, in the event of a conflict of interest, the mediator may undertake, or, if already engaged, continue to perform, mediation duties only with the express consent of the parties and provided he or she is certain of being able to conduct the mediation in a manner that does not undermine the independence of the proceedings.

A conflict of interest exists, in particular, in cases of:

a) a personal or professional relationship between the mediator and one of the parties or their legal representatives, or receipt in the past of a fee for services provided to any of the parties;

b) any financial or other interest, direct or indirect, deriving from the outcome of the mediation;

c) involvement of the mediator, in any way, in the subject matter of the dispute;

d) past conduct of the mediator, or of an associate of the mediator, or of another executive of the company for which he or she works, representing one of the parties in a capacity other than that of mediator;

e) any form of professional cooperation of the mediator with natural or legal persons involved in providing advice to one of the participating parties on matters concerning the subject matter of the mediation.

  1. After the conclusion of the mediation, and irrespective of its outcome, the mediator may not deal, in another professional capacity, with the specific case he or she handled, between the same parties.

Article 15 – Principle of the free will of the parties

  1. The mediator is obliged to conduct the mediation on the basis of the principle of the parties' private autonomy. The mediator ensures that the parties understand the characteristics of the procedure to be followed, as well as his or her own role and that of all participants, and informs the parties that they are free, at any time, to withdraw from the proceedings without any justification, sanction or penalty.

  2. The mediator ensures, in particular, that before the mediation proceedings begin, the parties have expressly agreed on the terms and conditions for submitting their dispute to mediation, including in particular the provisions governing the mediator's and the parties' obligation of confidentiality.

  3. The mediator ensures the proper conduct of the mediation proceedings.

  4. The mediator may terminate the mediation proceedings, after reasoned notice to the parties, where: a) a settlement of the dispute is being reached in a manner contrary to good morals or public policy, or b) the mediator considers that the continuation of the mediation is absolutely incapable of leading to a settlement of the dispute.

  5. The mediator takes every appropriate measure to ensure that the solution found for settling the dispute is the product of the parties' informed and considered consent, and that the parties understand the terms of the agreement.

  6. The mediator must inform the parties of how, where possible, they may render their agreement enforceable.

Article 16 – Confidentiality

The mediator is obliged to keep confidential the information arising from the mediation or in relation thereto, including the fact that mediation is to take place or has taken place, unless required to act otherwise by a legal provision, or for reasons of public policy, or unless the parties expressly consent to the disclosure of the information.

Article 17 – Disciplinary law

A. General principles

  1. Disciplinary proceedings are autonomous and independent of any other proceedings.

  2. Disciplinary sanctions are imposed by the Ethics and Disciplinary Control Committee and by the Central Mediation Committee.

  3. No one may be prosecuted twice for the same disciplinary offence, for which only one disciplinary sanction is imposed. A new disciplinary prosecution for the same offence is inadmissible.

  4. The removal, in any manner, of the criminally punishable character of an act, or the total or partial removal of the consequences of a criminal conviction, does not remove the disciplinary character of the act.

B. Disciplinary offences

  1. A disciplinary offence may be committed by an act or omission of the mediator in the course of his or her duties, contrary to the obligations arising from this law and inextricably linked to mediation, as well as from the Code of Conduct for Accredited Mediators.

  2. Disciplinary offences of the mediator include, in particular, the use of his or her status for the pursuit of unlawful purposes and, generally, undignified or improper conduct.

  3. Any felony (kakourghima) committed by a mediator, as well as any intentional misdemeanour (plimmelima) incompatible with the status of mediator, constitutes an autonomous disciplinary offence.

  4. Failure by the mediator to observe the principle of impartiality and confidentiality constitutes a serious disciplinary offence.

C. Limitation

  1. Disciplinary offences are time-barred two (2) years after their commission.

  2. The limitation period is suspended for a period not exceeding three (3) months upon the submission of a disciplinary complaint to the Ethics and Disciplinary Control Committee, by the parties or by any third party taking part in the mediation proceedings, or by the President of the Central Mediation Committee, as soon as he or she receives a complaint alleging disciplinarily reprehensible conduct by a mediator, or otherwise becomes aware of such conduct.

D. Disciplinary sanctions

  1. Disciplinary sanctions are: a) reprimand (advice); b) written censure; c) temporary suspension of accreditation for up to one (1) year; d) permanent revocation of accreditation.

  2. The sanction of permanent revocation of accreditation is imposed only in particularly serious cases of disciplinary offence. Such conditions exist, in particular, where the mediator: a) has been irrevocably convicted of a felony or of any intentional misdemeanour incompatible with the institution of mediation; b) has been repeatedly sanctioned with temporary suspension of accreditation for at least six (6) months in total within a three-year period.

  3. Where the offence is due to slight negligence, the competent disciplinary body may refrain from imposing a sanction, having regard to the circumstances in which the offence was committed.

E. Consequences of revocation of accreditation

  1. A mediator on whom a permanent or temporary revocation of accreditation has been imposed may not act as a mediator for as long as the sanction lasts.

  2. The validity of a successful outcome of mediation and of the agreement concluded is not affected by the sanction imposed on the mediator.

  3. The Central Mediation Committee must update the registers under Article 29 of this law regarding the temporary or permanent revocation of the mediator's accreditation from his or her duties.

F. Disciplinary bodies

  1. The Ethics and Disciplinary Control Committee, appointed by the Central Mediation Committee, is divided into a single-member first-instance committee and a three-member second-instance committee, with an equal number of alternate members. The term of office of its members is two years and may be renewed. A member who issued the first-instance disciplinary decision may not be a member of the second-instance committee. If the Ethics and Disciplinary Control Committee considers that the sanction of permanent revocation of accreditation should be imposed, competence rests with the Central Mediation Committee sitting in Plenary Assembly.

  2. The provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure on the recusal and disqualification of judges apply by analogy.

  3. An application for disqualification is inadmissible where it concerns so many members of the disciplinary body that its lawful composition becomes impossible.

  4. On every specific matter of disciplinary control, the Central Mediation Committee decides, and its decisions are executed under the responsibility of its President or of the member specifically designated by the President.

  5. For the submission of a complaint against a mediator, payment of a fee of thirty (30) euros is required, on pain of inadmissibility, the amount of which is adjusted by joint decision of the Ministers of Justice and Finance.

Article 18 – Mediator's fee

  1. The mediator's fee is freely determined by written agreement between the mediator and the parties.

  2. In the absence of a written agreement, the mediator's fee is determined as follows: a) in the cases under Article 6 of this law, the initiating party advances to the mediator the sum of fifty (50.00) euros as the fee for the mandatory initial session. This sum is borne by the parties in equal shares. Where the dispute is brought before a court, a party who did not attend the mediation proceedings, although duly summoned to do so under Article 7 of this law, or who did not pay its share of the mediator's fee for the mandatory initial session, is ordered, pursuant to Articles 176 et seq. of the Code of Civil Procedure, to pay the initiating party the entire amount it paid for the mandatory initial session. That sum is treated as a court cost irrespective of the outcome of the case; b) for each hour of mediation following the mandatory initial session, the minimum fee is set at eighty (80.00) euros and is borne by the parties in equal shares.

  3. The amounts under paragraph 2 may be adjusted by decision of the Minister of Justice.

  4. The mediator must provide the parties with full information on his or her fee in accordance with paragraph 2.

Article 19 – Business Activity Code (KAD)

The exercise of the profession of mediator on an exclusive basis alone is permitted. By decision of the Minister of Finance, a Business Activity Code (Κωδικός Αριθμός Δραστηριότητας, ΚΑΔ) shall be established for the profession of accredited mediator, together with the detailed rules for its application, for those wishing to practise it and not already holding another such code.

Article 20 – Associations of mediators

Associations of accredited mediators, for the purpose of providing mediation services, are established and operate with the participation of mediators, in accordance with the provisions in force from time to time.

Article 21 – Mediators' activity reports

Every accredited mediator is required, every four months of each calendar year and within the following fifteen (15) days after the end of that period, to inform the Central Mediation Committee of his or her activity, which the Committee reviews. Notification is made solely under the mediator's responsibility, on his or her individual page maintained on the website of the Central Mediation Committee. The Activity Report must include, at a minimum, the number of mediations conducted by the mediator, the outcome and duration of each mediation, the nature of the case, and whether or not the record was filed with the competent court, pursuant to paragraph 4 of Article 7 and paragraph 1 of Article 8 of this law. The obligation to submit the Activity Report applies to every mediator, regardless of whether or not mediations were conducted within the four-month period.

Article 22 – Training bodies

  1. A mediator training body (hereinafter the "Body"), operating under a licence granted following a specific and reasoned decision of the Central Mediation Committee, is:

A. A legal person governed by private law, the right to establish which belongs to: a) a bar association, or several jointly; b) one or more bar associations in cooperation with scientific, educational or professional bodies or chambers. In the cases under (a) and (b), cooperation is also possible with a foreign training body of recognised standing and of international recognition and experience in providing mediation training and, more generally, in alternative dispute resolution methods or in conducting mediations;

B. A Centre for Lifelong Learning (Κ.Ε.ΔΙ.ΒΙ.Μ.) of a Higher Education Institution (Α.Ε.Ι.), which has a relevant programme and whose operation is governed exclusively by the provisions in force from time to time on the operation of Α.Ε.Ι., provided that all the requirements of this law are met as regards trainer qualifications, training in mediation, and the minimum number of trainers and trainees;

C. A natural or legal person established in accordance with the legislation in force in Greece, or in the legislation of a Member State, whose main purpose is to provide training in mediation and other alternative dispute resolution methods.

The above Bodies are licensed in accordance with Articles 23 to 25 of this law.

  1. The Body has administrative and financial autonomy, except for the Centres for Lifelong Learning.

  2. The Body must employ, as a minimum: a) one (1) Director of the Body, and b) one (1) Training Director. These must hold a domestic tertiary education qualification, or an equivalent qualification from a foreign institution.

  3. The Body must employ administrative staff for secretarial support.

  4. The purpose of the Body is: a) to design programmes and provide basic training services of at least eighty (80) hours; b) to design further-training programmes, beyond the eighty (80) hours of basic training for prospective mediators, for the further acquisition of the knowledge and skills necessary to practise mediation and for their continuing professional development.

  5. Training Bodies must engage at least three (3) trainers, in order to provide quality training. Trainers must be accredited mediators and must either hold a master's or doctoral degree in the field of out-of-court dispute resolution or a related scientific discipline (in particular law, economics, management or social sciences), or have at least one hundred sixty (160) hours of demonstrated further training beyond basic training in mediation, or at least one hundred sixty (160) hours of demonstrated experience as mediator trainers. Trainers must also have four years' professional experience in their field, must have taken part in mediation proceedings as a mediator, mediator assistant or legal representative, and must have taken part in conferences, seminars and research programmes related to mediation.

  6. The number of prospective mediators participating in each training cycle of basic-training programmes may not exceed twenty-one (21). There must be no fewer than two (2) trainers per training cycle.

Article 23 – Licensing of Training Bodies

  1. The Body concerned, in order to obtain an operating licence, submits to the Central Mediation Committee a file comprising:

a) its application for licensing; b) a certified copy of its business-commencement certificate from the competent Tax Office (this requirement does not apply to Κ.Ε.ΔΙ.ΒΙ.Μ. of Α.Ε.Ι.); c) a list of the teaching staff, with their curricula vitae, accompanied by certified evidence of educational qualifications and training or professional experience, such as service certificates, attestations from bodies, official documents of professional experience, and a list of the remaining staff; d) any lawful document evidencing possession, holding, use or ownership of the training premises; e) a floor plan of the training premises, signed by an engineer, showing that the premises have at least three separate teaching rooms, in particular two rooms suitable for conducting lessons and simulations and one lecture room, together with a depiction of accessibility for persons with disabilities (the minimum area of teaching rooms must be fifteen (15) square metres, plus one and a half (1.5) square metres per trainee); f) a statutory declaration under Law 1599/1986, signed by the applicant or, in the case of a legal person, by its legal representative, certifying that the teaching rooms meet the applicable safety requirements, are suitable for the purpose of training, and have adequate technical infrastructure, in particular the minimum required equipment, comprising: i. an overhead projector and a projection screen, or any other audio-visual projection device; ii. a board in each room; iii. a photocopying machine; iv. a television or a computer; v. tables and seating for each room, according to its capacity; g) a fire-safety certificate for use as a training establishment, in accordance with the provisions in force; h) a civil-liability and guarantee insurance contract for an amount of at least two hundred thousand (200,000) euros; i) a fee of one thousand five hundred (1,500) euros, the amount of which may be adjusted by joint decision of the Ministers of Finance and Justice.

Bodies already licensed are deemed to satisfy the requirements of this law and their licence remains valid. In any event, such Bodies must submit any additional supporting documents required under this law within a reasonable period from its publication.

  1. The Central Mediation Committee, within two (2) months of the filing of a complete file with the supporting documents, checks its completeness. If the documents are incomplete, the Body concerned is requested in writing to send, within thirty (30) calendar days of the notification, the required documents duly completed. Upon receipt, the documents are re-examined and the Central Mediation Committee rules on the completeness of the file. The above check also applies to Bodies already licensed.

  2. Where the Body concerned either fails to submit the documents within the above thirty (30)-day period, or the documents submitted are incomplete as provided for in paragraph 1 and Articles 24 and 25 of this law, the application is rejected by a reasoned decision of the Central Mediation Committee. The Body concerned, whose application has been rejected, may resubmit a new application.

  3. The Body concerned, whose file is found to be complete, is subject to evaluation. Its evaluation is carried out by decision of the Central Mediation Committee, on the basis of the following five criteria: a) organisation, operation; b) scientific and other staff; c) technical infrastructure; d) training programme; e) adequacy of the training premises.

  4. Following successful evaluation of the complete file, the Body concerned is granted an operating licence; otherwise its application is rejected by a specifically reasoned decision of the Central Mediation Committee.

  5. Every year, within the first fifteen days of July, Bodies submit to the Central Mediation Committee a detailed report on their operation; the Committee reviews and evaluates the situation in accordance with this law and, in case of deficiency, imposes the sanctions under paragraph 8.

  6. If it is found that inaccurate information was declared or filed for the licensing and operation of the Body, the Central Mediation Committee permanently revokes the Body's operating licence.

  7. In case of failure by the Body to comply with its statutory obligations during its operating phase, the Central Mediation Committee imposes, by reasoned decision, depending on the seriousness of the breach, the degree of fault and any repetition, one of the following sanctions: a) a recommendation to comply; b) temporary suspension of the Body's licence from one (1) to six (6) months; c) permanent revocation of the Body's licence.

  8. By decision of the Minister of Justice, the terms and conditions for licensing and operating mediator training bodies may be amended.

Article 24 – Licensing of natural persons

The Body concerned, where it is a natural person, in addition to the documents under Article 23 of this law, submits:

a) a statutory declaration under Law 1599/1986, with certified signature, stating: aa) that the applicant does not hold the status of public employee, employee of a legal person governed by public law, or employee of a local authority; bb) that he or she has not been irrevocably convicted or committed for trial under Articles 8 and 9 of the Civil Servants' Code (Law 3528/2007, Α΄ 26), as amended and in force; cc) that he or she has not been dismissed from a post as a public employee or private-school teacher for disciplinary reasons; dd) that no administrative sanction of revocation of an establishment or operating licence has been imposed on him or her, in respect of his or her own or another private education and training body, within the last decade; and ee) his or her place of permanent residence;

b) social-insurance clearance (as employer and as insured person);

c) tax clearance;

d) a certificate from the Court of First Instance that no petition for a declaration of bankruptcy of the natural person has been filed and that he or she has not been declared bankrupt, or an equivalent document from the competent authority of a foreign State, where such a document is provided for under that State's law.

Article 25 – Licensing of legal persons

  1. The Body concerned, where it is a legal person, in addition to the documents under Article 23 of this law, submits: a) a certified copy of its articles of association, clearly showing its purpose, and a recent certificate of amendments from the General Commercial Registry (Γ.Ε.ΜΗ.); b) social-insurance clearance; c) tax clearance; d) a certificate from the Court of First Instance that no petition for a declaration of bankruptcy of the legal person has been filed and that it has not been declared bankrupt, or an equivalent document from the competent authority of the Member State in which the legal person has its seat, where such a document is provided for under that State's law.

  2. The legal representative of the legal person submits: a) a statutory declaration under Law 1599/1986, with certified signature, stating: aa) that he or she does not hold the status of public employee, employee of a legal person governed by public law, or employee of a local authority; bb) that he or she has not been irrevocably convicted or committed for trial under Articles 8 and 9 of the Civil Servants' Code (Law 3528/2007, Α΄ 26), as amended and in force; cc) that he or she has not been dismissed from a post as a public employee or private-school teacher for disciplinary reasons; dd) that no administrative sanction of revocation of an establishment or operating licence has been imposed on him or her, in respect of his or her own or another private education and training body, within the last decade; ee) that no such administrative sanction has been imposed on the legal person, in respect of its own or another private education and training body, within the last decade; and ff) his or her place of permanent residence; b) social-insurance clearance of the representative (as employer and as insured person); c) tax clearance of the representative; d) a certificate from the Court of First Instance that no petition for a declaration of bankruptcy of the natural person has been filed and that he or she has not been declared bankrupt, or an equivalent document from the competent authority of a foreign State, where such a document is provided for under that State's law.

Article 26 – Prospective mediators

Prospective mediators are entitled to apply to a Body for participation in basic-training programmes, provided they hold a qualification from a domestic higher-education institution, or an equivalent qualification from a foreign institution of recognised standing, and produce a criminal-record extract demonstrating that none of the impediments under Article 8 of Law 3528/2007 (Α΄ 26) applies to them.

Article 27 – Curriculum

A. The minimum content of the basic training curriculum and the examination syllabus for prospective mediators requires at least eighty (80) hours of instruction, of which at least fifty (50) hours must be attended in person, and consists of the following:

• Mediation and other forms of alternative dispute resolution. The international development of the institution. • Directive 2008/52/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 May 2008 on certain aspects of mediation in civil and commercial matters. • Fundamental characteristics, basic concepts and principles, and the definition of the institution of mediation under Greek and European law. • Scope of application – conditions for submitting disputes to mediation. • Methods of recourse to mediation – agreement to submit to mediation – consequences. • Procedure for conducting mediation. Stages. Legal representatives and other persons. • Mediation record – enforceability. • The mediator – role – liability of the mediator. • Disciplinary and Ethics Code. • Mediation skills and techniques – negotiation and communication techniques – basic concepts of psychology in mediation. • Mediation simulations. Their practical application. • Fundamental concepts of private law. • General commercial law, companies, negotiable instruments.

Judicial officers, lawyers and notaries may be exempted from attending the legal-subject courses of the curriculum under this article, namely the fundamental concepts of private law, general commercial law, companies, and negotiable instruments.

B. Further training of mediators. The further training of accredited mediators consists of compulsory additional training, every three years, of a minimum duration of twenty (20) hours, provided by licensed bodies or by recognised foreign bodies.

Article 28 – Accreditation of mediators

  1. The accreditation of mediators and their registration in the Registers under Article 29 of this law are carried out by the Central Mediation Committee following examinations. Mediators already accredited on the date this law enters into force retain their accreditation.

  2. Examinations of prospective mediators are held at least twice a year by the Examinations Committee, as appointed by the Central Mediation Committee. Examinations are written and oral and include an assessment based on simulations.

B. (Manner of conducting the examinations, criteria, conditions and terms — reconstructed from verified extracts of the source; see the caution note in the "Fonte" section):

a) The place, time and manner of conducting the examinations are determined by decision of the Examinations Committee, which is notified to licensed training bodies and posted on the relevant website of the Ministry of Justice at least thirty (30) days before they take place.

b) A condition of participation is the submission of an application, accompanied by a certificate from the training body attesting to the completion of basic training, together with a fee of one hundred (100) euros payable to the State.

c) The written examinations comprise seventy (70) multiple-choice questions and two (2) short-answer questions of up to one hundred fifty (150) words. Results are announced within thirty (30) days of the examination.

d) Candidates absent at the start of the examination are excluded. Scripts are initialled by a member of the Committee and bear no signature or other distinguishing mark other than the candidate's personal particulars, which are shown by identity card and are then covered with opaque tape.

e) Candidates who pass the written examination are called, within thirty (30) working days, to an oral examination, consisting of an assessment of mediation techniques through role-play and simulations.

f) Candidates are deemed to have passed if they obtain an average score of at least seventy per cent (70%) across the two examinations, with no score below fifty per cent (50%) in either examination.

g) The decision of the Examinations Committee is notified to the Central Mediation Committee, which accredits the candidates who have finally passed.

h) A prospective mediator who fails three (3) examination sessions must submit a new training certificate in order to sit the examination again.

i) Unsuccessful candidates may view their results within fifteen (15) days, without any right to re-marking; after that period, the written scripts and oral assessment records are destroyed.

j) The Central Mediation Committee has competence to resolve any question arising as to the application or interpretation of this article.

Article 29 – Public information – Mediators' Registers

  1. The Central Mediation Committee provides information on the institution of mediation and on how to access mediators' services.

  2. The Central Mediation Committee compiles and maintains, in electronic form, the following Mediators' Registers, published on the website of the Ministry of Justice: a) the General Register of Mediators, in which all accredited mediators throughout the country are registered in strict alphabetical order; and b) the Special Register of Mediators, in which accredited mediators based within the district of each Court of First Instance in the country are registered in ascending order of accreditation registration number.

  3. Each accredited mediator is assigned a unique registration number.

  4. The Mediators' Registers must contain the following information on each accredited mediator: a) personal identification particulars, e-mail address, other contact details, and registration number; b) the type of main professional activity, the basic qualification held, and any postgraduate qualifications; c) the Mediator Training Body, its training curriculum, and the hours of instruction of its programme; d) further training and other scientific activities related to mediation; and e) the categories of cases undertaken.

  5. The mediator must promptly notify the Central Mediation Committee of any change to the above personal particulars; the Committee verifies them and may call on the mediator to provide clarifications.

  6. A mediator accredited in another Member State of the European Union, complying with the provisions in force in that Member State in order lawfully to practise the profession of mediator, is registered in the Mediators' Registers upon application. The application is accompanied by the following documents: a) proof of the applicant's nationality; b) a copy of the certificates of competence or of the training qualification giving access to the profession of mediator in the other Member State; c) a certificate of any professional experience of the applicant in mediation. The Central Mediation Committee examines the documents produced by the applicant and may call on him or her to provide clarifications regarding his or her training.

  7. By decision of the Minister of Justice, the detailed rules for keeping the Registers under this article may be determined, and additional Registers may be created for a more complete organisation of the institution, such as a Register of Legal-Aid Mediators, a Register of Trainers, a Register of Mediators accredited abroad, and others.

Article 30 – Voluntary resignation – Suspension from mediation duties

  1. Every mediator has the right to resign from that status. The application for resignation is submitted electronically to the Central Mediation Committee, which removes the mediator from the Registers under Article 29 of this law. From the date the relevant application is filed, the mediator's duties and obligations cease by operation of law, except for the obligation to submit the Activity Report for the period during which he or she performed those duties.

  2. A mediator who has resigned from his or her duties may be re-registered in the Registers under Article 29 of this law, provided that five (5) years have not elapsed since the resignation. For re-registration, he or she must submit an application electronically to the Central Mediation Committee and must complete the minimum period of further training, in accordance with this law.

  3. Re-registration is not permitted for a mediator who has lost that status where a sanction of permanent revocation of accreditation has been imposed on him or her.

  4. The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article also apply where a mediator wishes to suspend his or her duties for any reason.

  5. In the above cases of acceptance of resignation, suspension of duties, or reappointment, the issuance of an administrative act is not required, and a corresponding note in the Mediators' Registers suffices. In the event of the death of an accredited mediator, he or she is removed from the Registers under this law upon the mere production of a death certificate, without the need for any further administrative act.

Article 31 – Legal aid

The provisions of Law 3226/2004 (Α΄ 24) on the provision of legal aid to low-income citizens apply by analogy to mediators and legal representatives under Article 2. By joint decision of the Ministers of Finance and Justice, the procedure for payment, the conditions and the amount of the compensation of such persons shall be determined, together with any other specific matter concerning the application of this article. By a similar decision, the compensation shall also be determined for persons under this article who have already been appointed after the entry into force of this law and before the publication of the decision of the Minister of Justice on the creation of the Register of Legal-Aid Mediators, pursuant to paragraph 7 of Article 29.

Article 44 – Entry into force

This law enters into force upon its publication in the Government Gazette, except for Articles 6 and 7, which enter into force and apply to actions filed after the lapse of the following dates:

a) from 15 January 2020 for family disputes, except those under points (a), (b) and (c) of paragraph 1, and those under paragraph 2 of Article 592 of the Code of Civil Procedure; and

b) from 15 March 2020 for disputes heard under the ordinary procedure and falling within the subject-matter jurisdiction of the Single-Member Court of First Instance, if the value of the subject matter of the dispute exceeds thirty thousand (30,000) euros, and of the Multi-Member Court of First Instance.

After two (2) years from the latter date, the Ministry of Justice shall evaluate the results of the institution of mediation and of the mandatory initial session under Articles 6 and 7 of this law.

Traduzione francese

AVIS IMPORTANT — TRADUCTION NON OFFICIELLE. Le texte ci-dessous est une traduction en langue française, établie à des fins purement informatives et pédagogiques, de la loi grecque 4640/2019 relative à la médiation en matière civile et commerciale, dans sa version consolidée résultant des lois modificatives indiquées dans la section « Fonte ». Cette traduction n'est pas officielle, n'est pas certifiée et n'a aucune valeur juridique ni contraignante. Elle ne remplace en aucune manière le texte original en langue grecque, publié dans l'Εφημερίδα της Κυβερνήσεως (Journal officiel de la République hellénique), seul texte faisant foi. Pour tout usage juridique, professionnel ou judiciaire, il convient de se référer exclusivement au texte grec officiel et, le cas échéant, de recourir à une traduction assermentée.

Article 1er – Objet

La présente loi a pour objet de régir l'institution de la médiation en matière civile et commerciale, ainsi que d'harmoniser davantage la législation grecque avec les dispositions de la directive 2008/52/CE du Parlement européen et du Conseil du 21 mai 2008 sur certains aspects de la médiation en matière civile et commerciale, de caractère national ou transfrontalier. Les dispositions de la présente loi ne s'appliquent pas à la médiation judiciaire (δικαστική μεσολάβηση), telle que régie par le Code de procédure civile.

Article 2 – Définitions

Aux fins de la présente loi, les définitions suivantes s'appliquent :

  1. Par « litige privé », on entend la contestation relative à l'existence, à l'étendue, au contenu ou aux titulaires d'un droit privé ; par « droits privés », on entend ceux reconnus par le droit privé.

  2. Par « médiation », on entend un processus structuré, quelle qu'en soit la dénomination, caractérisé par la confidentialité et l'autonomie privée, dans lequel deux parties ou plus tentent volontairement, en agissant de bonne foi et avec loyauté dans leurs échanges, de résoudre un litige les opposant au moyen d'un accord, avec l'aide d'un médiateur.

  3. Par « médiateur », on entend un tiers, par rapport aux parties participantes et au litige, qui s'engage à mener la médiation de manière appropriée, efficace et impartiale, en aidant les parties à trouver une solution mutuellement acceptable à leur litige.

  4. Par « représentant légal », on entend l'avocat mandataire de chaque partie, qui assiste à la procédure de médiation conformément aux dispositions de la présente loi et fournit des conseils juridiques à son mandant.

  5. Par « séance initiale obligatoire de médiation », on entend la séance entre le médiateur et les parties, qui a lieu obligatoirement dans les cas de litiges privés prévus au paragraphe 1 de l'article 6 de la présente loi, avant l'audience de l'affaire devant le Tribunal. Au cours de la séance initiale obligatoire, le médiateur informe les parties de la procédure de médiation et des principes fondamentaux qui la régissent, ainsi que de la possibilité d'un règlement extrajudiciaire de leur litige compte tenu de ses particularités et de sa nature.

  6. Par « litige transfrontalier », on entend celui dans lequel au moins une des parties a son domicile permanent ou sa résidence habituelle dans un État membre autre que celui de toute autre partie, à la date à laquelle : a) les parties conviennent de recourir à la médiation, après la survenance du litige ; b) la médiation a été ordonnée par une juridiction d'un État membre ; c) une obligation de médiation existe en vertu du droit national ; ou d) les parties ont été invitées à y recourir par la juridiction compétente. Est également considéré comme litige transfrontalier celui dans lequel, à l'issue de la procédure de médiation, des procédures judiciaires ou arbitrales entre les parties sont engagées dans un État membre autre que celui du domicile permanent ou de la résidence habituelle des parties à la date à laquelle sont survenues les situations visées aux points a), b) ou c) du présent paragraphe.

  7. Par « clause de médiation », on entend l'accord écrit des parties de recourir à la médiation, relatif à des litiges futurs et se référant à un rapport juridique déterminé dont ces litiges seront issus, à condition que les parties aient le pouvoir de disposer de l'objet des litiges, conformément aux dispositions du droit substantiel.

Article 3 – Litiges susceptibles de médiation – Obligation d'information à la charge de l'avocat mandataire

  1. Peuvent être soumis à la procédure de médiation les litiges civils et commerciaux, de caractère national ou transfrontalier, existants ou futurs, à condition que les parties aient le pouvoir de disposer de l'objet du litige conformément aux dispositions du droit substantiel.

  2. Avant de saisir le Tribunal, l'avocat mandataire est tenu d'informer par écrit son mandant de la possibilité de régler le litige, ou une partie de celui-ci, par la médiation conformément au paragraphe 1, ainsi que de l'obligation de recourir à la séance initiale obligatoire et à la procédure y afférente prévue aux articles 6 et 7 de la présente loi. Le document d'information est rempli et signé par le mandant et son avocat mandataire et est déposé avec l'acte introductif de l'action éventuellement intentée, ou avec les conclusions, au plus tard avant l'audience, sous peine d'irrecevabilité de l'audience de l'action. Cette disposition s'applique également aux actions déposées du 30.11.2019 à ce jour.

Article 4 – Recours à la médiation

  1. Le recours à la médiation pour les litiges qui y sont soumis en vertu de la présente loi est autorisé : a) si les parties conviennent de recourir à la procédure de médiation après la survenance du litige ; b) si les parties sont invitées à recourir à la médiation et y consentent, conformément au paragraphe 2 ; c) si le recours à la médiation est ordonné par une autorité judiciaire d'un autre État membre et que la soumission du litige n'est pas contraire aux bonnes mœurs et à l'ordre public ; d) si le recours à la procédure de médiation est imposé par la loi ; e) si un accord écrit des parties comporte une clause de médiation.

  2. Le Tribunal devant lequel est pendant un litige privé susceptible d'être soumis à la procédure de médiation conformément à l'article 3 de la présente loi peut, à tout stade de l'instance, selon le cas, en tenant compte, selon son appréciation souveraine, de toutes les circonstances de l'affaire, inviter les parties à recourir à la procédure de médiation pour résoudre le litige. Si les parties y consentent, l'accord écrit correspondant est consigné dans le procès-verbal du Tribunal. Dans ce cas, le Tribunal renvoie obligatoirement l'audience de l'affaire à une date fixée après l'écoulement de trois mois et au plus tard dans les six mois, sans que soit computée la période des vacances judiciaires. La même conséquence s'applique dans les autres cas de recours à la médiation pendant l'instance. Lorsque les parties au litige, ou l'une d'elles, comparaissent devant le Tribunal par l'intermédiaire d'un avocat mandataire, le mandat conféré à celui-ci couvre également l'accord relatif à la soumission du litige à la médiation.

  3. La soumission d'un litige de droit privé à la procédure de médiation n'exclut pas l'adoption d'une mesure conservatoire y afférente, conformément aux dispositions du Code de procédure civile. Le juge qui ordonne la mesure conservatoire peut fixer, conformément au paragraphe 1 de l'article 693 du Code de procédure civile, un délai, non inférieur à trois (3) mois, pour l'introduction de l'action au fond.

  4. Le procureur près le tribunal de première instance, dans l'exercice de ses compétences conformément au point a) du paragraphe 4 de l'article 25 de la loi 1756/1988 (Α΄ 35), a le droit de recommander, lorsque cela est possible, aux parties en litige le recours à la procédure de médiation.

  5. L'accord des parties sur le recours à la procédure de médiation est régi par les dispositions du droit substantiel des contrats et doit en décrire l'objet.

Article 5 – Procédure de médiation et honoraires du représentant légal

  1. Dans la procédure de médiation, les parties comparaissent assistées de leur représentant légal, à l'exception des litiges de consommation et des litiges de faible importance, dans lesquels la comparution personnelle des parties est autorisée. Un tiers peut également participer à la procédure, si cela est jugé nécessaire d'un commun accord entre les parties et le médiateur. Les honoraires du représentant légal de chaque partie sont librement convenus et, pour sa participation à l'ensemble de la procédure de médiation, il est délivré, conformément au Code des avocats, un reçu d'avance de cotisations d'un montant de soixante (60,00) euros pour les affaires relevant de la compétence du tribunal d'instance (Ειρηνοδικείο), de cent (100,00) euros pour les affaires relevant de la compétence du tribunal de première instance à juge unique et de cent cinquante (150,00) euros pour les affaires relevant de la compétence du tribunal de première instance à formation collégiale. Ces montants peuvent être révisés par décision du ministre de la Justice.

  2. Le médiateur est désigné par les parties, ou par un tiers de leur choix commun, y compris les centres de médiation. Il n'y a qu'un (1) médiateur, sauf si les parties conviennent par écrit qu'il y en aura plusieurs.

  3. Le moment, le lieu et les autres modalités procédurales du déroulement de la médiation sont fixés par le médiateur en accord avec les parties. S'il n'est pas possible que les deux parties et le médiateur soient physiquement présents au même endroit et au même moment, la médiation peut se dérouler par vidéoconférence via ordinateur ou tout autre système de vidéoconférence, accessible aux autres parties au litige.

  4. Le médiateur peut, dans l'exercice de ses fonctions, communiquer avec chacune des parties et les rencontrer soit séparément, soit conjointement. Les informations recueillies par le médiateur lors de ses contacts avec une partie ne sont pas communiquées à l'autre partie sans l'accord de la partie qui les a fournies.

  5. La procédure de médiation revêt, en principe, un caractère confidentiel ; il n'en est tenu aucun procès-verbal et elle doit se dérouler de manière à ne pas porter atteinte à cette confidentialité, sauf accord contraire des parties. Avant le début de la procédure, tous les participants s'engagent par écrit à respecter la confidentialité de la procédure de médiation. La même obligation s'impose à tout tiers participant à la procédure. Les parties, si elles le souhaitent, s'engagent par écrit à garder également confidentiel le contenu de l'accord auquel elles pourraient parvenir au cours de la médiation, sauf si sa divulgation est nécessaire à son exécution conformément au paragraphe 4 de l'article 8, ou si elle est imposée pour des raisons d'ordre public.

  6. Lorsque le litige est porté devant les tribunaux ou soumis à l'arbitrage, le médiateur, les parties, leurs représentants légaux et toute personne ayant participé de quelque manière que ce soit à la procédure de médiation ne sont pas entendus comme témoins et ne peuvent produire d'éléments issus de la procédure de médiation ou s'y rapportant, notamment se référer aux discussions, déclarations et propositions des parties, ni aux opinions du médiateur, sauf lorsque cela est imposé pour des raisons d'ordre public, notamment pour assurer la protection des mineurs ou pour éviter le risque d'atteinte à l'intégrité physique ou à la santé psychique d'une personne.

  7. Dans l'exercice de ses fonctions, le médiateur n'engage sa responsabilité civile qu'en cas de dol.

Article 6 – Séance initiale obligatoire de médiation – Litiges soumis

  1. Les litiges civils et commerciaux suivants sont soumis à la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation, pour autant que les parties aient le pouvoir de disposer de l'objet de leur litige :

a) les litiges familiaux, à l'exception de ceux visés aux points a), b) et c) du paragraphe 1, ainsi que ceux visés au paragraphe 2 de l'article 592 du Code de procédure civile ;

b) les litiges jugés selon la procédure ordinaire et relevant de la compétence matérielle du tribunal de première instance à juge unique, si la valeur de l'objet du litige excède trente mille (30 000) euros, et du tribunal de première instance à formation collégiale, conformément aux dispositions du Code de procédure civile ;

c) les litiges pour lesquels un accord écrit des parties prévoit une clause de médiation en vigueur.

Dans les cas ci-dessus, aux fins de la recevabilité de l'audience de l'action éventuellement intentée, le procès-verbal de la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation doit être déposé avec les conclusions pour l'audience de l'affaire.

  1. Sont exclus de la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation prévue au paragraphe 1 les litiges dans lesquels l'État, une collectivité locale (Ο.Τ.Α.) ou une personne morale de droit public (Ν.Π.Δ.Δ.) est partie.

  2. Le recours des parties à la médiation judiciaire ne les dispense pas de la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation.

Article 7 – Procédure de la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation et honoraires du représentant légal

  1. Pour les litiges visés au paragraphe 1 de l'article 6 de la présente loi, le médiateur est désigné selon les modalités prévues au paragraphe 2 de l'article 5 de la présente loi. En particulier, la partie diligente a la faculté soit de contacter l'autre ou les autres parties au litige en vue de la nomination d'un médiateur accepté d'un commun accord, soit de s'adresser à un médiateur de son choix. Dans ce dernier cas, le médiateur contacte l'autre ou les autres parties par tout moyen approprié pour vérifier si un accord se dégage sur sa personne, et en reçoit l'approbation écrite. Si la communication ne peut être établie, ou si aucun accord n'est trouvé sur la personne du médiateur, un médiateur est alors désigné par la Commission centrale de médiation, à la diligence de la partie diligente. La désignation s'opère par ordre de priorité, sur la base du numéro d'ordre au Registre spécial des médiateurs visé à l'article 29 de la présente loi, parmi les personnes résidant dans le ressort du tribunal territorialement compétent pour connaître du litige. Lorsque le nombre de médiateurs disponibles est insuffisant, ou en cas de conflit d'intérêts, la désignation s'opère parmi les personnes résidant dans le ressort de la cour d'appel concernée. En cas de choix du médiateur par la Commission centrale de médiation, celui-ci est tenu, dans un délai de trois (3) jours ouvrables, de déclarer s'il accepte sa désignation. Si ce délai de trois (3) jours ouvrables expire sans réponse, l'absence d'acceptation est présumée. En cas de non-acceptation de la désignation, le médiateur suivant par ordre de priorité est choisi sur le Registre spécial précité.

  2. La partie diligente soumet au médiateur désigné par les parties ou par la Commission centrale de médiation une demande de recours à la procédure de médiation, en lui adressant, par voie électronique ou par tout autre moyen approprié, un formulaire complété, sur lequel doivent obligatoirement figurer les coordonnées des parties conformément au point 3 de l'article 118 du Code de procédure civile, ainsi que l'objet du litige, et en reçoit un accusé de réception. Le médiateur communique par tout moyen approprié avec les parties afin de fixer la date et le lieu de la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation. À défaut d'accord, le médiateur fixe lui-même la date et le lieu de la séance initiale obligatoire. Dans les deux cas, il notifie ces éléments par écrit aux parties au moins cinq (5) jours avant la séance initiale obligatoire, par lettre recommandée ou par voie électronique, et en reçoit un accusé de réception. Les frais de notification sont avancés par la partie diligente et sont liquidés au titre des dépens, en cas de procès ultérieur.

  3. La séance initiale obligatoire de médiation a lieu au plus tard dans les vingt (20) jours suivant le lendemain de l'envoi au médiateur, par la partie diligente, de la demande de recours à la procédure de médiation. Si l'une des parties réside à l'étranger, ce délai est prorogé jusqu'au trentième (30e) jour suivant le lendemain de l'envoi de la demande au médiateur. La séance initiale obligatoire de médiation revêt un caractère confidentiel, il n'en est tenu aucun procès-verbal, et les paragraphes 4, 6 et 7 de l'article 5 de la présente loi s'appliquent par analogie.

  4. À l'issue de la séance initiale obligatoire, un procès-verbal est établi par le médiateur, signé par celui-ci et par tous les participants ; si une action est intentée par la suite, ou si elle l'a déjà été, ce procès-verbal est déposé au tribunal, sous peine d'irrecevabilité de l'audience de l'affaire, avec les conclusions. Ce procès-verbal doit obligatoirement indiquer le mode de notification de la séance initiale obligatoire aux parties et leur participation ou non à celle-ci.

  5. À la séance initiale obligatoire, les parties comparaissent assistées d'un représentant légal, dont les honoraires sont librement convenus. Dans le cas des personnes morales, le représentant légal peut désigner un mandataire par procuration avec légalisation de la signature. À titre exceptionnel, est admise à la séance initiale obligatoire, en vertu d'une procuration, la seule participation du représentant légal de la partie dont la présence physique n'est manifestement pas possible, notamment dans les cas où elle rencontre des difficultés de déplacement en raison d'une maladie grave, ou lorsqu'aucune vidéoconférence par ordinateur ou autre système de vidéoconférence n'est disponible, ou lorsqu'elle réside à l'étranger.

  6. Le tribunal saisi du litige peut infliger à la partie qui ne s'est pas présentée à la séance initiale obligatoire de médiation, bien qu'ayant été régulièrement convoquée à cet effet conformément au présent article, une amende qui ne peut être inférieure à cent (100) euros ni supérieure à cinq cents (500) euros, compte tenu de son comportement général et des motifs de sa non-comparution. Les amendes visées à la phrase précédente sont versées au Fonds pour l'administration de la justice (ΤΑ.Χ.ΔΙ.Κ.), auquel une copie de la décision est communiquée à la diligence du greffier du Tribunal, et peuvent être révisées par décision du ministre de la Justice. Le recours contre la décision, en ce qui concerne l'amende, n'est recevable que s'il porte également sur le fond de l'affaire. Une décision conjointe des ministres de la Justice et des Finances détermine la procédure de constatation et de recouvrement de l'amende et règle toute autre question relative à l'application du présent paragraphe. Cette procédure s'applique également aux amendes déjà infligées.

  7. Si les parties décident de poursuivre la procédure de médiation, avec le même médiateur ou avec un médiateur différent, un accord écrit de soumission du litige à la procédure de médiation est établi et la procédure de l'article 5 de la présente loi est suivie ; celle-ci doit être menée à son terme dans un délai de quarante (40) jours, calculé à compter du lendemain de l'expiration du délai de vingt ou trente jours susmentionné. Les parties peuvent convenir de proroger le délai de quarante (40) jours. La période du 1er au 31 août n'est pas comptée dans les délais susmentionnés.

Article 8 – Force exécutoire des accords issus de la médiation

  1. Le médiateur établit un procès-verbal de médiation qui doit contenir : a) le nom et le numéro d'identification fiscale du médiateur ; b) la date et le lieu où s'est déroulée la médiation ; c) les coordonnées complètes des parties ayant eu recours à la médiation et les noms de leurs représentants légaux ; d) une référence à l'accord ou aux modalités particulières selon lesquelles les parties ont eu recours à la médiation, conformément au paragraphe 1 de l'article 4 de la présente loi ; e) les coordonnées complètes de toute autre personne ayant participé de quelque manière que ce soit à la procédure de médiation ; et f) l'accord auquel sont parvenues les parties au cours de la médiation, ou le constat de l'absence d'accord.

  2. À l'issue de la procédure de médiation, le procès-verbal est signé par le médiateur, les parties et leurs représentants légaux. En cas d'absence d'accord, le procès-verbal peut être signé par le seul médiateur. Chaque partie peut déposer le procès-verbal constatant l'accord à tout moment auprès du greffe du tribunal matériellement et territorialement compétent, devant lequel l'affaire est pendante ou doit être portée. Après le dépôt du procès-verbal auprès du Tribunal, l'introduction d'une action pour le même litige est irrecevable dans la mesure où son objet est couvert par l'accord des parties, et toute instance pendante est éteinte. Lors du dépôt, un droit de cinquante (50,00) euros doit être acquitté, dont le montant peut être révisé par décision conjointe des ministres des Finances et de la Justice. La charge de ce droit incombe à la partie qui procède au dépôt, sauf accord contraire des parties.

  3. Le procès-verbal de médiation visé au présent article constitue, à compter de son dépôt auprès du greffe du tribunal compétent conformément au paragraphe 2, un titre exécutoire au sens du point ζ' du paragraphe 2 de l'article 904 du Code de procédure civile, pour autant que l'accord soit susceptible de faire l'objet d'une exécution forcée. La grosse est délivrée à titre gratuit par le juge ou le président du tribunal compétent conformément au paragraphe 2.

  4. Si l'accord contenu dans le procès-verbal de médiation comprend également des dispositions relatives à des actes juridiques devant, en vertu de la loi, revêtir la forme notariée, ces actes doivent être établis sous cette forme. Dans ce cas, s'appliquent les règles régissant l'établissement de tels actes notariés et leur transcription.

  5. Le procès-verbal de médiation visé au présent article, à compter de son dépôt auprès du greffe du tribunal compétent conformément au paragraphe 2, peut être utilisé comme titre pour l'inscription ou la radiation d'une hypothèque, conformément au point c) du paragraphe 1 de l'article 293 du Code de procédure civile.

Article 9 – Effets de la médiation sur la prescription, les délais de forclusion et les délais procéduraux

  1. La notification écrite du médiateur aux parties relative à la tenue de la séance initiale obligatoire, ou l'accord de recourir volontairement à la procédure de médiation prévue à l'article 5, suspend la prescription et la forclusion pour l'exercice des prétentions et des droits, pour autant que celles-ci aient déjà commencé à courir conformément aux dispositions du droit substantiel, ainsi que les délais procéduraux des articles 237 et 238 du Code de procédure civile, pendant toute la durée de la procédure de médiation.

  2. Sous réserve des dispositions des articles 261, 262 et 263 du Code civil, la prescription et la forclusion pour l'exercice des prétentions et des droits de droit substantiel qui avaient été suspendues reprennent leur cours le lendemain de l'établissement du procès-verbal de non-accord, ou de la notification à l'autre partie et au médiateur d'une déclaration de retrait de la procédure de médiation par l'une des parties, ou de l'achèvement ou de la cessation, de quelque manière que ce soit, de la procédure de médiation. Si l'accord contenu dans le procès-verbal de médiation comporte des conditions, des délais ou toute autre clause dont dépend l'exercice des droits résultant de l'accord, la prescription et la forclusion suspendues reprennent leur cours à compter de la réalisation de la condition ou de la clause, ou de l'expiration du délai.

  3. Les délais procéduraux visés au paragraphe 1 reprennent leur cours à compter de l'établissement du procès-verbal de non-accord, ou de la notification à l'autre partie et au médiateur d'une déclaration de retrait de la procédure de médiation par l'une des parties, ou de l'achèvement ou de la cessation, de quelque manière que ce soit, de la procédure de médiation. En cas d'accord, la reprise des délais procéduraux se justifie pour les éléments de la cause non couverts par l'accord des parties.

Article 10 – Commission centrale de médiation

  1. La Commission centrale de médiation est composée de treize (13) membres et est constituée, par décision du ministre de la Justice, de :

a) deux (2) magistrats de l'ordre civil et pénal, dont un (1) au moins du grade de conseiller à la Cour de cassation (Areios Pagos) ou grade équivalent, en activité ou honoraire, et un (1) au moins du grade de président de tribunal de première instance, conseiller de cour d'appel ou président de cour d'appel ou grade équivalent, ayant une expérience ou une spécialisation et, de préférence, une accréditation en médiation, désignés après avis du Conseil supérieur de la magistrature, s'ils sont en activité ;

b) deux (2) professeurs d'établissements d'enseignement supérieur, en activité ou émérites, ayant une expérience ou une spécialisation en médiation et, de préférence, une accréditation, dont l'un au moins ayant été professeur de faculté de droit du pays ;

c) deux (2) représentants de l'Assemblée plénière des ordres des avocats du pays, ayant une expérience ou une spécialisation en médiation et, de préférence, une accréditation, après avis conforme de l'Assemblée plénière des ordres des avocats ;

d) deux (2) représentants du ministère de la Justice, choisis parmi les agents en fonction à l'administration centrale de grade au moins Β΄, ou parmi les juristes détachés au cabinet politique du ministre ou du secrétaire d'État à la Justice, ou du secrétaire général ;

e) deux (2) médiateurs représentant des organismes professionnels du pays, à la suite d'un appel à manifestation d'intérêt publié sur le site internet du ministère de la Justice ;

f) trois (3) médiateurs, à la suite d'un appel à manifestation d'intérêt publié sur le site internet du ministère de la Justice, justifiant d'au moins quatre années d'expérience professionnelle dans leur domaine ainsi que d'une expérience en médiation, résultant soit de leur participation à la formation des médiateurs, soit de leur participation à des procédures de médiation en qualité de médiateurs, d'assistants du médiateur ou de représentants légaux, soit de leur participation à des congrès, séminaires et programmes de recherche relatifs à la médiation.

  1. Le président de la Commission centrale de médiation est le magistrat du grade le plus élevé et, entre pairs de même grade, le plus ancien.

  2. Par la décision visée au paragraphe 1, un membre suppléant est désigné pour chaque membre titulaire de la Commission centrale de médiation.

  3. Il est interdit aux membres de la Commission centrale de médiation, ainsi qu'aux tiers nommés membres de la sous-commission visée au point d) du paragraphe 3 de l'article 11 de la présente loi, d'entretenir une quelconque relation de collaboration avec des organismes de formation visés par la présente loi.

  4. Le mandat du président et des membres est de trois ans et peut être renouvelé une fois. Le président et les membres de la Commission centrale de médiation sont remplacés lorsqu'ils ont été nommés en violation du paragraphe 4.

  5. Les fonctions de secrétaire de la Commission centrale de médiation sont exercées par un ou plusieurs agents de l'administration centrale du ministère de la Justice, des tribunaux ou d'un autre service public ou personne morale de droit public, mis à disposition ou détachés à temps plein et exclusif de leur service auprès de la Commission centrale de médiation. Ce détachement est décidé, par dérogation à toute autre disposition, par décision du ministre de la Justice, à sa demande, après avis du président de la Commission centrale de médiation et avis conforme du conseil de service compétent à l'égard de l'agent concerné.

Article 11 – Compétences de la Commission centrale de médiation

La Commission centrale de médiation est compétente pour connaître de toute question relative au contrôle de l'application de l'institution de la médiation.

La Commission centrale de médiation peut constituer, à sa discrétion, des sous-commissions pour le règlement rapide et le contrôle des questions découlant de l'application de la présente loi. Ces sous-commissions sont composées de membres de la Commission centrale de médiation, sans limitation quant à la participation d'un même membre à plus d'une sous-commission. Ces sous-commissions sont expressément habilitées par la Commission centrale de médiation à régler définitivement les questions dont elles sont saisies, sauf lorsque la présente loi prévoit spécifiquement la compétence de l'Assemblée plénière de la Commission centrale de médiation.

En tout état de cause, quatre (4) sous-commissions sont obligatoirement constituées au sein de la Commission centrale de médiation, pour un mandat de deux ans, ayant les compétences suivantes :

a) la « Commission du registre des médiateurs », compétente pour la tenue des registres prévus à l'article 29 de la présente loi, pour toute question y afférente ou pour l'adoption d'actes relatifs aux registres tenus, ainsi que pour le recueil des rapports annuels d'activité prévus à l'article 21 de la présente loi ;

b) la « Commission de déontologie et de contrôle disciplinaire », compétente pour le respect par les médiateurs des obligations découlant de la présente loi et pour l'application du droit disciplinaire et l'imposition des sanctions disciplinaires. La constitution de cette sous-commission s'effectue conformément au paragraphe Ε.1 de l'article 17 de la présente loi ;

c) la « Commission de contrôle des organismes de formation », compétente pour toute question relative aux organismes de formation des médiateurs ;
